> 38: 
> 39:       const { data, error } = await query;
> 40:       if (error) throw error;
> 41:       return data as DesignArtifactRef[];

> 43:     enabled: !!user?.id,
> 44:   });

> 50:       const res = await fetch('http://127.0.0.1:7456/api/projects');

> 1: # OE-661-ROADMAP: Rumo ao Milhão (30-Day Plan)
> 2: 
> 3: ## 1. Liquidação de Dívidas Críticas
> 4: 
> 5: ### Dívida Financeira (Owner: engineer)
> 6: **Status:** As Edge Functions de assinatura estão parametrizadas para as chaves `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY` e `STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET`.
> 7: **O que falta para o Swap (Sandbox -> Produção):** 
> 8: 1. Provisionar as chaves live da Stripe via Supabase CLI (`supabase secrets set STRIPE_SECRET_KEY=sk_live_...`).
> 9: 2. Trocar os Product IDs/Price IDs no frontend/banco de dados para refletirem os produtos reais da Stripe.
> 10: 3. Virar a chave do webhook URL no painel da Stripe para o domínio real.
> 11: 
> 12: ### Dívida de Postback (Owner: scientist)
> 13: **Status:** A Edge Function `handle-ml-postback` mapeia o `revenue_impact` e cria um nodo de `observation`, mas falha em criar a aresta topológica (`mcorch_edges`) ligando o evento ao conteúdo.
> 14: **Por que a aresta ATTRIBUTES_REVENUE_TO não recebe dados:**
> 15: O script de postback não executa o `insert` na tabela `mcorch_edges` com a relação `ATTRIBUTES_REVENUE_TO` para o nodo `content_mesh_asset`. Apenas atualiza o score estático do nó.
> 16: 
> 17: ### Dívida de Memória (Owner: main)
> 18: **Status:** A Skill `.claude/skills/gateway-recovery.md` foi consolidada, documentando o bypass da restrição de `sessions_spawn` e o saneamento do PATH do daemon via Systemd.
> 19: 
> 20: ### Dívida de Resiliência de IA — Fallback de LLM (Owner: engineer)
> 21: **OTD-LLM-FALLBACK** (SLA: 2026-06-07):
> 22: Implementar fallback em cascata para completions/chat em todas as ~17 Edge Functions. Quando o OpenRouter principal estiver sem créditos (402) ou indisponível, o sistema deve automaticamente cair para o Gemini Free (via `GEMINI_API_KEY` do vault) e, se necessário, para modelos `:free` do OpenRouter.
> 23:

**Analyzing the Roadmap**

I've started with `roadmap-v1.md`, focusing on the "Rumo ao Milhão" plan. My immediate concern is the user's desire to integrate OpenDesign natively ("como algo nativo"). The user wants a specific visual integration as depicted in the second screenshot. I'm unpacking the implications of this native implementation. The primary goal is to match the provided screenshot as closely as possible.


**Prioritizing the Redesign**

I'm now focusing on integrating the MIV design system. A priority task will be added to redesign the UI, following MIV guidelines, specifically to `sprint-priorities.md`. The user's request is prompting me to plan a redesign of the frontend, incorporating OpenDesign natively, which requires architectural changes. My plan will start with the reconfiguration of `CanvasDesignPage.tsx`.


**Simplifying the Implementation**

I'm now investigating a simpler approach. Instead of complex integrations, I'm considering making `CanvasDesignPage.tsx` load the OpenDesign dashboard directly in a fullscreen iframe. This would provide a native feel, removing extra tabs and local UI elements, without creating new balance components. OpenDesign manages project loading and creation internally. Now I'm reviewing `CanvasDesignPage.tsx` to ensure critical functionality is retained with this design.


**Confirming the Native Integration**

I've confirmed the OpenDesign app provides all the necessary sidebar functionalities within its own UI running at port 7456. My revised plan is to make the iframe fill the entire page content and point to `http://127.0.0.1:7456/`, achieving the "native" look in the second screenshot. The embedded UI handles project loading, creation, and editing through its own SPA routing; navigating to a specific project like `/projects/<project_id>` updates only the iframe's internal URL, and the parent URL is unchanged. The current implementation in `CanvasDesignPage.tsx` will be adapted to handle the full-page iframe.


**Refining the Fullscreen Integration**

Okay, I've just realized a simpler approach. I can change `CanvasDesignPage.tsx` to display a full-screen iframe pointing directly to `http://127.0.0.1:7456`, removing the need for `selectedProjectId` in the source. With this change, OpenDesign will manage the project itself, giving the user the native visual experience they are after. This approach will not disrupt other existing code.


**Validating the Implementation**

Now I'm completely sure this is the most streamlined approach! By setting `w-full h-full` on both the inner `div` and the `iframe`, I'm confirming that the iframe will automatically resize to precisely fill the viewport. This is because the parent container already uses `flex-1 h-full overflow-hidden`. The existing layout in `DashboardLayout.tsx` enables this perfect integration. It's a clean replacement.

<summary>💭 Thinking Process</summary>

**Discovering a key comment**

I've just stumbled upon a crucial comment, buried in line 5720. It's an insightful note on the Entry view structure, which I find very helpful for my current analysis. This "left sidebar + right tabs" description provides a great high-level overview.


**Analyzing the Redesign Layout**

I'm now diving deeper into the redesigned entry view. I'm focusing on the left sidebar, specifically trying to reconcile the user's comment about the "avatar-menu" with the new architecture. I've realized that the old `.entry-side` is likely deprecated, replaced by the `.entry-nav-rail`. I'm verifying this by checking the DOM and also cross-checking the `EntryNavRail.tsx` to find a place for the avatar menu, and I found it! I found that it has a `footer` section.


**Locating the avatar menu**

I've just tracked down the `avatarMenu` within `EntryShell.tsx`, which renders a settings button. This is rendered within `EntryNavRail`. I've confirmed that the `AvatarMenu` component (agent switcher/settings dropdown) is used in the project view header. However, in the entry view, an `InlineModelSwitcher` is used instead, and the settings cog is just a button in the top right. I'm now examining the user's comment to see how this all might relate to the structure of `EntryNavRail.tsx`.
